** =sessions.list=

** =sessions.start=

Accept a host launch or recreate; retry keys return the original acceptance, not current readiness. For API-1 compatibility typed clients omit the empty host options map; nonempty maps remain present and are refused by host policy.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "key": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/OperationKey"
    },
    "configuration": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchManifest"
    },
    "name": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchConfigurationName",
      "default": ""
    },
    "agent_name": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchAgentName"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "cwd": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/HostPath"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "scope": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchScope"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "dev_shell": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "rows": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchDimension"
    },
    "cols": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchDimension"
    },
    "detached": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "default": false
    },
    "raid": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "recreate": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "options": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/HostLaunchOptions",
      "default": {}
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "key",
    "configuration",
    "rows",
    "cols"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "description": "name is required/nonempty for an ordinary launch and empty/omitted for recreate. Recreate forbids explicit agent_name,cwd,scope,dev_shell,raid; it resolves the record and retries before ordinary validation. cwd must be absolute; dev_shell is an absolute PATH[#ATTR] reference; raid has the existing raid-name grammar. Domain validators retain diagnostics and refusal order."
}
#+end_src

** =terminals.create=

Host create accepts ordered positional parameters session,argv,cwd,env,name,rows,cols,notify or a closed named object; only session is required. Sandbox create/list/close accept a closed named object or null-as-empty, never positional arrays, and may never supply session. The existing sandbox relay derives the session from the connection and preserves policy/refusal ordering. Host notify=true is rejected before command/name/size validation; sandbox notify may request an exit notice. Size defaults independently to 40 rows and 120 columns. Command/name/size, target/policy/init, capacity and uniqueness checks precede canonical init encoding and spawn transfer.

** =terminals.list=

Terminal records expose id, nullable name, display label, host/agent origin, Unix-millisecond started_at, rows/cols and attached. IDs increase per session and are never reused; list orders started terminals oldest first, including host and agent origins. Creation answers only after Spawned; pending creates do not appear in lists. Listed records remain until the program ends. Snapshots retain their domain record bridge until C15. An exact retained session ID reads published terminal records even after stop (empty after terminal cleanup). Agent names resolve only while the runtime is live: a released name or unretained ID returns -32004, and a reused name selects the new live session.

** =terminals.close=

Close requests cancellation of the terminal process group and returns accepted=true. The entry remains until exit. Sandbox callers can close only agent-created terminals; host-created ones return -32009. An explicit close suppresses exit notices; disconnect while creating does not cancel the already requested terminal. Existing lifecycle/notice ownership stays handwritten.

** =terminals.read=

Read/send/wait first validate command-specific input, then resolve a live session/terminal; pending waits remain connection-owned, count toward the per-session limit of 16 and are withdrawn on disconnect. A connection serves no other call while waiting. Host detach ends only the frontend and keeps the program alive; the frontend stream remains reserved until it closes. Resize validates 1..500 rows/cols before target resolution and updates the existing screen/PTY through runtime commands. Sandbox attach/detach/resize remain unavailable and never enter its callable allowlist. Host read/send/wait accept named objects or positional arrays in their field order: session,terminal then source,lines for read; text,keys for send; match,regex,exit,timeout,lines for wait. Only session and terminal are required structurally. Sandbox calls omit session and retain object-only/null-as-empty rules. Send needs a string text (even empty) or nonempty keys; empty/null text and keys are distinguished. Wait selects one nonempty match string, Rust regex string, or exit=true; match/regex allow exit absent/null/false. Source, line/timeout/text/key bounds and regex compilation remain existing semantic validators in their original order. Request constraints separately describe cross-field requirements without changing generated record/default decoding. Read returns text,alternate_screen,rows,cols and optional truncated=true. Wait returns matched+line, timeout, or exited+nullable code/signal+text; truncated=true is present only when output is cut. Matching final output precedes exit reporting. Existing 256 KiB text and 4 KiB line limits remain. Legacy CLI readers independently normalize string text/status/line and signed 64-bit code/signal; missing/malformed/non-object values retain their old fallback, with unknown wait status reported as timeout. Send/detach/resize callers ignore successful result bodies. Current producers use generated precise DTOs; private runtime relays stay JSON until their owning migration.

** =permissions.decide=

Approve or deny a pending request by immutable session, request and approval identities. All fields are required; named or ordered [session,request,approval,approved] forms are retained, unknown fields/null params are refused. String selectors are not prevalidated as identifiers: existing record lookup and identity/state checks determine absent versus stale errors. A located raid-owner request is refused before stale-identity checks; use raid methods instead. A dev shell approval requires its prepared preview identity; denial can cancel evaluation. Live requester/cancellation and runtime capacity checks retain their order. The result acknowledges the decision, not grant readiness, and echoes request/approved. No RPC parameter selects the deciding actor; the host endpoint supplies it.

** =permissions.list=

List retained permission requests in insertion order. Exact optional session-ID/state string filters are conjunctive; they do not resolve names or reject unknown strings. Named filters can be absent/null, while the original positional [session,state] requires both slots, which may be null. No filter returns all retained records. Host-only after initialization. Reads retain finalized records independently of session lifetime and do not mutate approvals or preview state. Required nullable record keys and omitted host-only optionals preserve their current wire presence. Legacy readers retain package as the missing-kind default, open record fields, arbitrary future kind/state strings, opaque preview JSON, and original positional record order; Snapshot consumers use the generated compatible reader through an explicit domain bridge until C15.

** =host.list=

List retained host-command records in insertion order, including finalized jobs. Host sees all sessions; the host CLI list/cancel commands print the original successful JSON. Both routes require initialization and retain existing shutdown/liveness, capacity and cancellation ordering. Request bodies retain the endpoint 16384-byte limit. Active/no-outcome views always include eight fields, including nullable message/name/started_at; cached terminal views additionally include nullable exit_code/signal/code together. The record state, name and timestamp override cached outcome display fields, while log remains the sandbox path. Client readers preserve legacy independent field fallbacks or ignored bodies.

** =raids.invite=

Checks raid syntax, 1..16 entries and every target resolution/liveness before cloning/committing membership. The first invited member owns a newly created raid; duplicate targets retain existing idempotent behavior. Host-only request shape; both session IDs and live names are resolved where a session selector exists. Requests retain original positional order, closed named fields and optional null id/generation guards. Wire DTO decoding keeps plain strings so existing domain validation/error precedence is unchanged. Event capacity is checked before commits and remains -32010. Raid/member records contain no host paths, terminal endpoints or process identities. Member rows retain join order and omit vanished sessions. Current owner/paused invariants remain enforced by the pure raid domain rules. The host body limit is 16384 bytes. CLI mutations ignore successful bodies and print original JSON; members uses an independent legacy display projection without tightening malformed/additive peer replies.

** =messages.send=

Authenticated host mailbox mutation. Closed named parameters and original positional forms; null params refused. The caller-chosen key identifies an exact retry, recovered before target/liveness resolution. Caller identity never comes from parameters. Existing semantic validation keeps 1..64 ASCII identifier keys/IDs, positive claim generations, recipient/reason limits of 512 UTF-8 bytes, and bodies (including empty strings) up to 8192 UTF-8 bytes (schema x-max-bytes records these limits; runtime semantic enforcement stays handwritten, including escaped JSON). Each request uses the existing 65536-byte frame limit. Shared unpaused logged raid membership authorizes goblin routes, while host routes remain independent. Audit acceptance precedes publication; stale claims, capacity, audit failures, no-reply notices, name reuse, and deterministic retry behavior stay in the state machine. Successful CLI bodies remain uninterpreted and are printed unchanged, with the local operation key added only by the CLI. Send CLI omits absent conversation; raw API accepts absent/null conversation.

** =inbox.requeue=

Authenticated host mailbox mutation. Closed named parameters and original positional forms; null params refused. The caller-chosen key identifies an exact retry, recovered before target/liveness resolution. Caller identity never comes from parameters. Existing semantic validation keeps 1..64 ASCII identifier keys/IDs, positive claim generations, recipient/reason limits of 512 UTF-8 bytes, and bodies (including empty strings) up to 8192 UTF-8 bytes (schema x-max-bytes records these limits; runtime semantic enforcement stays handwritten, including escaped JSON). Each request uses the existing 65536-byte frame limit. Shared unpaused logged raid membership authorizes goblin routes, while host routes remain independent. Audit acceptance precedes publication; stale claims, capacity, audit failures, no-reply notices, name reuse, and deterministic retry behavior stay in the state machine. Successful CLI bodies remain uninterpreted and are printed unchanged, with the local operation key added only by the CLI. Send CLI omits absent conversation; raw API accepts absent/null conversation.

** =messages.get=

Read only as the authenticated host caller. messages.get accepts any string ID structurally, then performs the existing visibility lookup (host sees all; a goblin sees messages it sent or received); malformed IDs remain absent/unavailable rather than becoming an earlier syntax error. inbox.status selects only the caller inbox and unfinished operations. ** =communications.list=

Host-only communications pagination. Closed named params default after=0 and limit=100, with optional nullable session. Original positional [after,limit,session] requires all three slots because session had no Serde sequence default. Decode first, then validate limit1..100 before resolving an exact session ID or a unique running agent name. Filter by actor or sessions, advance cursor through excluded events, and stop before advancing past an included event exceeding 100 rows or 512KiB of summed serialized event bytes. latest is the last global sequence, independently of the page/filter. Preserve offline regular-file, 128MiB, final newline, sequence and instance checks. Body limit64KiB. C13b owns event payload variants.

** =history.list=

Host-only read of recreation history, closed empty named/positional params. Decode first, then require an available store. Records sort descending(updated,id), omit deleting rows, and append current live-record running status. Sum each serialized record including running, stop before first record crossing900KiB and set truncated; wrapper bytes excluded. Offline CLI uses original unlocked loader, version normalization and running:null. No history file rewrite. Compatible CLI preserves raw partial/future records and empty fallback; Emacs readers remain permissive. Preserve original direct-Serde named and positional decoding, including excess-array length diagnostics and older_than/before/keep/dry_run field error precedence; do not normalize positional input into an object first.

** =history.prune=

Host-only prune. Closed params and positional[older_than?,before?,keep?,dry_run?], all defaulting to null/null/null/false. Decode before requiring at least one non-null criterion, then require store. Conjoin strict age/before thresholds and keep selection; saturating age subtraction, descending(updated,id) keep order, oldest-first output; exclude live and deleting records. Dry-run or empty selection replies immediately; real deletion waits for ordered durable writer completion and reports partial failures with dry_run:false. Existing receipt/connection lifecycle and store lock remain authoritative. CLI preserves requested dry_run wording; failed collection decoded before removed and malformed individual failed records retain dash fallback. Preserve original direct-Serde named and positional decoding, including excess-array length diagnostics and older_than/before/keep/dry_run field error precedence; do not normalize positional input into an object first.

    "failed"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

      "type": "array",
      "items": {}
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "removed",
    "failed"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

** =state.subscribe=

Host-only subscription. Decode original closed Empty params directly before checking an existing subscription; publish all earlier dirty mutations before assigning a fresh ID and taking the initial full snapshot. Sequence is global u64, including zero before publication. Client consumes its connection, sets nonblocking before validating subscription/sequence/snapshot, and retains original missing-field messages, compatible nested records and initial cross-instance acceptance. Ordinary host calls preserve the subscription; explicit unsubscribe and server.stop cancel it.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {},
  "required": [],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

Positional parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "array",
  "items": false,
  "minItems": 0,
  "maxItems": 0
}
#+end_src

  "type": "object",

      "type": "string"
    },
    "sequence": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 0,
      "maximum": 18446744073709551615,
      "format": "uint64",
      "x-json-integer": true
    },
    "snapshot": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/StateSnapshot"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "subscription",
    "sequence",
    "snapshot"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

** =state.unsubscribe=

Host-only explicit cancellation. Direct original Subscription Serde named/positional decoding precedes exact active-ID check; wrong/stale/repeated IDs return -32009. A successful call clears the subscription and returns accepted:true. Rust dropping a subscription closes its stream without sending this method.

** Notification =exec.signal=

exec.signal is a host-client-to-daemon notification, with no id, only during an active exec. Named {signal:I32} or positional [I32] accepts SIGHUP=1, SIGINT=2, SIGTERM=15. Missing, invalid or unsupported signals, an unexpected method, or any id close the active-exec connection; cleanup cancels its exec only. Outside active exec, all no-id client notifications are ignored after envelope parsing; a host request with id and method exec.signal is unknown (-32601). It is never a callable method or sandbox capability.

Direction: =client-to-server=. Sender: host client. Receiver: daemon. State: active exec only; ignored as no-id notification outside active exec.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "signal": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/ExecSignalNumber"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "signal"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

Positional parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "array",
  "items": [
    {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/ExecSignalNumber"
    }
  ],
  "minItems": 1,
  "maxItems": 1
}
#+end_src

** Notification =state.changed=

Full replacement snapshot emitted only after a dirty publication, after raid reconciliation and bounded eviction. All subscribers observe the same global next sequence. Keep unevictable active permissions/runtime authority; finalized permissions then oldest evictable runtime-less sessions are discarded until the serialized snapshot fits900KiB or no safe eviction remains. Existing encoded-frame/2MiB connection queue overflow disconnects a slow subscriber. Rust tick reads/consumes at most16384bytes per iteration, buffers partial frames, checks method/subscription/next sequence and instance, and commits each valid replacement before any later frame error. EOF is checked before buffered data. Extra outer fields/IDs remain accepted by the compatible Rust reader. Emacs keeps partial snapshots and its signed arbitrary integer sequence policy; no strict full payload decoder is added there.

Direction: =server-to-client=. Sender: daemon. Receiver: host subscriber. State: active subscription.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "subscription": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "sequence": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 0,
      "maximum": 18446744073709551615,
      "format": "uint64",
      "x-json-integer": true
    },
    "snapshot": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/StateSnapshot"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "subscription",
    "sequence",
    "snapshot"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

Positional parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "not": {}
}
#+end_src

** =terminals.create=

Host create accepts ordered positional parameters session,argv,cwd,env,name,rows,cols,notify or a closed named object; only session is required. Sandbox create/list/close accept a closed named object or null-as-empty, never positional arrays, and may never supply session. The existing sandbox relay derives the session from the connection and preserves policy/refusal ordering. Host notify=true is rejected before command/name/size validation; sandbox notify may request an exit notice. Size defaults independently to 40 rows and 120 columns. Command/name/size, target/policy/init, capacity and uniqueness checks precede canonical init encoding and spawn transfer.

** =terminals.list=

Terminal records expose id, nullable name, display label, host/agent origin, Unix-millisecond started_at, rows/cols and attached. IDs increase per session and are never reused; list orders started terminals oldest first, including host and agent origins. Creation answers only after Spawned; pending creates do not appear in lists. Listed records remain until the program ends. Snapshots retain their domain record bridge until C15.

** =terminals.close=

Close requests cancellation of the terminal process group and returns accepted=true. The entry remains until exit. Sandbox callers can close only agent-created terminals; host-created ones return -32009. An explicit close suppresses exit notices; disconnect while creating does not cancel the already requested terminal. Existing lifecycle/notice ownership stays handwritten.

** =terminals.read=

Read/send/wait first validate command-specific input, then resolve a live session/terminal; pending waits remain connection-owned, count toward the per-session limit of 16 and are withdrawn on disconnect. A connection serves no other call while waiting. Host detach ends only the frontend and keeps the program alive; the frontend stream remains reserved until it closes. Resize validates 1..500 rows/cols before target resolution and updates the existing screen/PTY through runtime commands. Sandbox attach/detach/resize remain unavailable and never enter its callable allowlist. Host read/send/wait accept named objects or positional arrays in their field order: session,terminal then source,lines for read; text,keys for send; match,regex,exit,timeout,lines for wait. Only session and terminal are required structurally. Sandbox calls omit session and retain object-only/null-as-empty rules. Send needs a string text (even empty) or nonempty keys; empty/null text and keys are distinguished. Wait selects one nonempty match string, Rust regex string, or exit=true; match/regex allow exit absent/null/false. Source, line/timeout/text/key bounds and regex compilation remain existing semantic validators in their original order. Request constraints separately describe cross-field requirements without changing generated record/default decoding. Read returns text,alternate_screen,rows,cols and optional truncated=true. Wait returns matched+line, timeout, or exited+nullable code/signal+text; truncated=true is present only when output is cut. Matching final output precedes exit reporting. Existing 256 KiB text and 4 KiB line limits remain. Legacy CLI readers independently normalize string text/status/line and signed 64-bit code/signal; missing/malformed/non-object values retain their old fallback, with unknown wait status reported as timeout. Send/detach/resize callers ignore successful result bodies. Current producers use generated precise DTOs; private runtime relays stay JSON until their owning migration.

** =terminals.wait=

Read/send/wait first validate command-specific input, then resolve a live session/terminal; pending waits remain connection-owned, count toward the per-session limit of 16 and are withdrawn on disconnect. A connection serves no other call while waiting. Host detach ends only the frontend and keeps the program alive; the frontend stream remains reserved until it closes. Resize validates 1..500 rows/cols before target resolution and updates the existing screen/PTY through runtime commands. Sandbox attach/detach/resize remain unavailable and never enter its callable allowlist. Host read/send/wait accept named objects or positional arrays in their field order: session,terminal then source,lines for read; text,keys for send; match,regex,exit,timeout,lines for wait. Only session and terminal are required structurally. Sandbox calls omit session and retain object-only/null-as-empty rules. Send needs a string text (even empty) or nonempty keys; empty/null text and keys are distinguished. Wait selects one nonempty match string, Rust regex string, or exit=true; match/regex allow exit absent/null/false. Source, line/timeout/text/key bounds and regex compilation remain existing semantic validators in their original order. Request constraints separately describe cross-field requirements without changing generated record/default decoding. Read returns text,alternate_screen,rows,cols and optional truncated=true. Wait returns matched+line, timeout, or exited+nullable code/signal+text; truncated=true is present only when output is cut. Matching final output precedes exit reporting. Existing 256 KiB text and 4 KiB line limits remain. Legacy CLI readers independently normalize string text/status/line and signed 64-bit code/signal; missing/malformed/non-object values retain their old fallback, with unknown wait status reported as timeout. Send/detach/resize callers ignore successful result bodies. Current producers use generated precise DTOs; private runtime relays stay JSON until their owning migration.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "terminal": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "match": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null
    },
    "regex": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/TerminalRegex"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null

    "exit": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "boolean"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null
    },
    "timeout": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/TerminalTimeout"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null
    },
    "lines": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/TerminalLines"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "terminal"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

** =flake.run=

Build an app from this sandbox’s trusted dev shell generation, mount its closure, and return its program. No new trust or approval is granted. App attributes are bounded ASCII names. Evaluation is bounded by the daemon; disconnect cancels work. The client has no response timeout and runs only an existing normalized store file. Trailing input is refused before parameter/policy validation.

** =devshell.diff=

Evaluate a read-only refresh preview. No approval request, candidate, GC root, mount or generation is changed; pending refresh candidates are left alone. Update and lock are mutually exclusive only when update is a non-null array and lock=true. Empty update means all inputs, absent/null update means no update. Requests accept named fields or [update,lock] with defaults, never null. Policy, trusted lock sources, evaluator limits and cancellation retain their existing order and ownership. Requires devshell-diff feature; client has no response timeout. Trailing input is refused before parameter and policy validation.

** =editor.open=

Open sandbox files in the configured host Emacs. Requires editor-open feature and editor policy. Requests are named or [files,wait]; each file also retains [path,line?,column?] with the original order/defaults. The generated DTO is the named representation; nested positional metadata normalizes wire arrays before decoding. Semantic file/position checks remain in the gate before policy; trailing input is refused first. The daemon derives every TRAMP filename and pins that host to the requesting session and daemon before opening, without a shell. At most eight pending edits per sandbox. Disconnect kills/reaps the editor client; stopping the goblin returns -32009. No response deadline is imposed by the CLI.

          }

      "minItems": 1,
      "maxItems": 32
    },
    "wait": {
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "files",
    "wait"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}

** =permissions.request=

Request a package, Docker engine, dev shell refresh or approved host command. Requires initialized, live caller authority; trailing input is refused before structural/semantic and policy checks. Body limit is 16384 bytes. Reasons are 1..1024 Unicode characters, not UTF-8 bytes. Named package defaults to empty; positional [kind,package,reason,update?,lock?,host?] requires the first three slots. Host commands retain nested [argv,cwd,env?,timeout?,detach?,name?] forms. Existing validators enforce kind-specific combinations, host-name diagnostics before general validity, command byte totals and policy/refusal order. Approval and completion are distinct; disconnect withdraws an attached request or cancels its host job, while detached host work survives and reports by notice. The daemon owns host path resolution/pinning, approvals, candidate identity and all descriptor/lifecycle state.

          "type": "null"

    "host": {

        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/HostCommandParams"
        },
        {
          "$ref": "#/x-positional-schemas/HostCommandParams"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "kind",

  "additionalProperties": false
}

Client result profile =grant-cli=:

The package/Docker/devshell CLI uses its original closed grant decoder, accepting named missing message or exactly [request,status,message], then requiring identifier request and ready/denied/error status. It emits message:null when absent. Unknown result fields, failed/withdrawn statuses and malformed types retain outcome-unknown exit 2.

** =host.list=

List retained host-command records in insertion order, including finalized jobs. Sandbox visibility is limited to the calling session; another session job and a non-host permission are indistinguishable from an absent job (-32004). The CLI prints original JSON for list/status/cancel; wait/logs use independent status/outcome fields. Both routes require initialization and retain existing shutdown/liveness, capacity and cancellation ordering. Request bodies retain the endpoint 4096-byte limit. Active/no-outcome views always include eight fields, including nullable message/name/started_at; cached terminal views additionally include nullable exit_code/signal/code together. The record state, name and timestamp override cached outcome display fields, while log remains the sandbox path. Client readers preserve legacy independent field fallbacks or ignored bodies.

** =raids.leave=

Leave the caller raid. A non-owner created by a raid owner cannot leave while its creation relationship holds; owners may leave and pause remaining members. The last member dissolves the raid. No caller-supplied target/raid is accepted. Sandbox parameter shapes differ from host methods. Named params are closed; original positional forms are retained and null params refused. Existing initialization/stopping, runtime liveness, created-member control, actor derivation, event capacity and 4096-byte body limit remain in force. ** =messages.send=

Authenticated sandbox mailbox mutation. Closed named parameters and original positional forms; null params refused. The caller-chosen key identifies an exact retry, recovered before target/liveness resolution. Caller identity never comes from parameters. Existing semantic validation keeps 1..64 ASCII identifier keys/IDs, positive claim generations, recipient/reason limits of 512 UTF-8 bytes, and bodies (including empty strings) up to 8192 UTF-8 bytes (schema x-max-bytes records these limits; runtime semantic enforcement stays handwritten, including escaped JSON). Each request uses the existing 65536-byte frame limit. Shared unpaused logged raid membership authorizes goblin routes, while host routes remain independent. Audit acceptance precedes publication; stale claims, capacity, audit failures, no-reply notices, name reuse, and deterministic retry behavior stay in the state machine. Successful CLI bodies remain uninterpreted and are printed unchanged, with the local operation key added only by the CLI. Send CLI omits absent conversation; raw API accepts absent/null conversation.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "key": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "to": {
      "type": "string"

** =messages.reply=

Authenticated sandbox mailbox mutation. Closed named parameters and original positional forms; null params refused. The caller-chosen key identifies an exact retry, recovered before target/liveness resolution. Caller identity never comes from parameters. Existing semantic validation keeps 1..64 ASCII identifier keys/IDs, positive claim generations, recipient/reason limits of 512 UTF-8 bytes, and bodies (including empty strings) up to 8192 UTF-8 bytes (schema x-max-bytes records these limits; runtime semantic enforcement stays handwritten, including escaped JSON). Each request uses the existing 65536-byte frame limit. Shared unpaused logged raid membership authorizes goblin routes, while host routes remain independent. Audit acceptance precedes publication; stale claims, capacity, audit failures, no-reply notices, name reuse, and deterministic retry behavior stay in the state machine. Successful CLI bodies remain uninterpreted and are printed unchanged, with the local operation key added only by the CLI. Send CLI omits absent conversation; raw API accepts absent/null conversation.

** =messages.get=

Read only as the authenticated sandbox caller. messages.get accepts any string ID structurally, then performs the existing visibility lookup (host sees all; a goblin sees messages it sent or received); malformed IDs remain absent/unavailable rather than becoming an earlier syntax error. inbox.status selects only the caller inbox and unfinished operations. Closed named params, original positional forms and null refusal retain their existing decoding and readiness order. Status remains readable after audit failure and includes the failure flags. Existing frame limit is 64 KiB.

  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

  "description": "Require all five original inbox fields so malformed/missing data cannot become an empty inbox. Additive raid_audit_failed/operations may be absent on older daemons, defaulting to false/empty. Wrong scalar/container types, float counters, tuple arity and u64 overflow are errors."
}

** Notification =host.submitted=

For attached permissions.request host-command work, the daemon sends this notification after the request is registered and before its terminal result. It identifies the sandbox output file. Detached submissions return pending acceptance and do not emit this notification. The host CLI legacy reader classifies by method only, even for nonstandard envelopes, and independently reads a string params.request or an empty string fallback. Preserve that behavior separately from the canonical closed producer params.

Direction: =server-to-client=. Sender: daemon. Receiver: sandbox host-command CLI. State: attached initialized permissions.request connection, before terminal result.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "request": {
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "request"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

Positional parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "not": {}
}
#+end_src

Legacy client classification follows =host-submitted-legacy-reader=; the normalized DTO is:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "request": {
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "request"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

* attach

** Notification =attach.hello=

attach.sock begins with a frontend-to-daemon attach.hello notification (no id); named and ordered version,rows,cols,terminal positional parameters retain the existing Serde forms. Version 1 and nonzero u16 dimensions are checked after structural decoding; bad version or zero dimensions retain the unsupported-version diagnostic. A supplied terminal requires 1..500 dimensions during target attachment, after the existing state/policy checks. Hello has a 4 KiB body and 3-second frame deadline, eight pending hellos per goblin and at most four accepts per tick. The daemon reads exactly the hello frame and consumes no following raw bytes.

Direction: =client-to-server=. Sender: frontend. Receiver: daemon attach.sock. State: first frame only, before raw terminal bytes.

Named parameters:

  "type": "object",
  "properties": {

    "terminal": {

        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ],
      "default": null
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "version",
    "rows",
    "cols"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

** Notification =attach.waiting=

While startup or required onAttach variables are pending, the daemon sends attach.waiting with a required missing array, initially/periodically at one-second intervals. Empty missing means startup/draining; nonempty names identify variables that must be fresh and nonempty this run. A live competing attachment refuses without resizing. An exited onAttach client retains its physical stream until the frontend closes; new hellos receive empty waiting notices during that drain.

Direction: =server-to-client=. Sender: daemon attach.sock. Receiver: frontend. State: after hello, before fixed-id completion.

Named parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "missing": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "string"
      }
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "missing"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
#+end_src

Positional parameters:

#+begin_src json
{
  "not": {}
}
#+end_src

Legacy client classification follows =attach-client-compatibility=; the normalized DTO is:

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "has_missing": {
      "type": "boolean"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "has_missing"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

** Completion exchange =attach.reply=

Completion after a valid modern hello and any waiting notifications. Modern attach.sock and legacy terminal.sock complete with a correlated JSON-RPC response of id 0: result attached=true, or error -32009 with a diagnostic. Only success switches the stream to raw bytes. terminal.sock is server-first with no hello; it retains stopped output, waits for configuration/readiness and refuses onAttach goblins with an upgrade message. Modern named-terminal attachments answer at once without waiting notices and redraw before live output. The generic runtime Terminal::attach retains its private caller-supplied id (including nonzero test ids); public entrypoints pass 0. Byte queues, refusal-before-resize, descriptor ownership, cancellation and framed/raw boundaries remain handwritten.

Socket: =attach.sock=. Direction: =server-to-client=. Fixed response ID: =0=.

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "attached": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "enum": [
        true
      ]
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "attached"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

Errors: -32009.

** Completion exchange =legacy.attach=

Server-first legacy completion; the frontend sends no hello. Modern attach.sock and legacy terminal.sock complete with a correlated JSON-RPC response of id 0: result attached=true, or error -32009 with a diagnostic. Only success switches the stream to raw bytes. terminal.sock is server-first with no hello; it retains stopped output, waits for configuration/readiness and refuses onAttach goblins with an upgrade message. Modern named-terminal attachments answer at once without waiting notices and redraw before live output. The generic runtime Terminal::attach retains its private caller-supplied id (including nonzero test ids); public entrypoints pass 0. Byte queues, refusal-before-resize, descriptor ownership, cancellation and framed/raw boundaries remain handwritten.

Socket: =terminal.sock=. Direction: =server-to-client=. Fixed response ID: =0=.

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "attached": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "enum": [
        true
      ]
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "attached"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true
}
#+end_src

Errors: -32009.

** Notification =SupervisorStart=

Bootstrap with complete generated Source/Handoff metadata. API is checked before Source decoding; listener descriptor allocation precedes handoff batching. Current producers emit every nullable field; the fieldwise legacy receiver accepts absent legacy_listener. Unknown outer fields reject before handoff validation, and overlap/count/descriptor checks retain their original order. Ownership adoption occurs only when the full handoff is complete.

Direction: =daemon-to-supervisor=. Sender: daemon. Receiver: supervisor. State: first frame; startup.

** Notification =Leases=

Continuation of one already pending handoff. Fields decode and unknown keys reject before pending-state lookup. Transfer must match; leases must be nonempty; count, more and cumulative bytes/descriptor references are checked before final adoption. No allocation is based on declared count. Either transfer-sending peer can emit these frames.

Direction: =bidirectional=. Sender: transfer-sending peer. Receiver: transfer-adopting peer. State: after a handoff header with more=true, until declared count is satisfied.

        "$ref": "#/definitions/SupervisorLease"
      }

    "more": {
      "type": "boolean"
    }

    "more"

}

* supervisor actor method phases

These gates inspect method identity before logical decoding. Pending identities name an existing gate without claiming migration of its payload.

#+begin_src json
[
  {
    "name": "entry",
    "x-rust-name": "Entry",
    "direction": "daemon-to-supervisor",
    "allowed": [
      "Setup",
      "Maintenance",
      "Preflight",
      "SupervisorStart"
    ],
    "x-dispatch-variants": {
      "Setup": "Setup",
      "Maintenance": "Maintenance",
      "Preflight": "Preflight",
      "SupervisorStart": "SupervisorStart"
    },
    "pending-identities": {
      "Setup": {
        "chunk": "C18",
        "reason": "Scope Setup payload contract remains separate; only the existing first-frame method identity is bound here."
      }
    },
    "consumer": "libs/supervisor/src/entry.rs:main",
    "description": "Classify the raw first method before general decoding or runtime startup. Missing, non-string or unknown methods retain the existing refusal; Setup retains its independent descriptor refusal."
  },
  {
    "name": "maintenance-first",
    "x-rust-name": "MaintenanceFirst",
    "direction": "daemon-to-supervisor",
    "allowed": [
      "Maintenance"
    ],
    "pending-identities": {},
    "consumer": "libs/supervisor/src/maintenance.rs:start",
    "description": "Reject any other raw first method and dispose descriptors before logical decode, cancellation setup or cleanup operations."
  },
  {
    "name": "preflight-adopted",
    "x-rust-name": "PreflightAdopted",
    "direction": "daemon-to-supervisor",
    "allowed": [
      "Adopted"
    ],
    "pending-identities": {},
    "consumer": "libs/supervisor/src/preflight.rs:start",
    "description": "After sending a rooted or unrooted manifest requiring adoption, only scalar Adopted reaches the decoder. Wrong-direction multipart data is dropped before pending receive state."
  },
  {
    "name": "manifest-result",
    "x-rust-name": "ManifestResult",
    "direction": "supervisor-to-daemon",
    "allowed": [
      "Event.Manifest",
      "PreflightFailed"
    ],
    "pending-identities": {},
    "consumer": "packages/daemon/src/supervised/oneshot.rs:Expected::allows",
    "description": "Only one single-frame manifest or operation failure may reach one-shot result decoding. Preserve raw descriptor disposal on wrong-direction rejection and all later result/ACK/EOF/reap validation."
  },
  {
    "name": "maintenance-result",
    "x-rust-name": "MaintenanceResult",
    "direction": "supervisor-to-daemon",
    "allowed": [
      "MaintenanceFinished"
    ],
    "pending-identities": {},
    "consumer": "packages/daemon/src/supervised/oneshot.rs:Expected::allows",
    "description": "Only the single-frame maintenance result reaches logical decoding. Existing fixed post-result deadline, EOF and successful reap requirements remain unchanged."
  }
]
#+end_src

* Supervisor generation boundaries

- =Params= :: Field-by-field extraction remains handwritten to preserve missing control field errors, finish-before-validation ordering, API checks and descriptor ownership. Generated DTOs are assembled only at the original extraction points.
- =Source and Handoff= :: Canonical bootstrap SourceSlot/HandoffSlot now reference generated complete Source/Handoff metadata. The separate SupervisorStartRead carries raw nested JSON only until original API-before-source and finish-before-handoff/overlap checks; generated nested decoders then run at those original points. Owned resource/manifest leases, descriptors, transfer guards and channel IO remain handwritten.
- =Result<(), String>= :: SupervisorMaintenanceOutcome maps to the standard Serde Result<(),String>, preserving exact enum errors and Ok:null / Err:string encoding.
- =MaintenanceOperation= :: Schema-driven tagged enum generation retains named variants/field order/optional-null emission and original Serde diagnostics; Typify replacements reference this generated enum.
- =Descriptor= :: Schema-driven explicit record generation retains the original Descriptor Rust name, public index usize field, $fd rename, Copy/Clone/Debug and exact Serde diagnostics, including standalone positional [index]. channel::references still requires exact object markers; standalone compatibility does not bypass transport scanning.
- =Recreate and Worktree= :: Generated wire-only Recreate and Worktree project explicitly to/from the original runtime domain records. Recreate ignores additive fields and defaults absent worktree; Worktree stays closed. No domain validation, paths, resource policy or history writer is moved.
- =Handoff dev_shell= :: The WireHandoff dev_shell field is an opaque serialized JSON String, not traversable JSON: serde_json::to_string/from_str of the existing runtime DevShell retains arbitrary lock-file JSON without exposing its $fd-looking keys to channel descriptor scanning. Inner domain decoding, including exact UnexpectedEof/error behavior, remains with the existing runtime decoder; its implementation is not a new transport.
- =Manifest catalogue= :: The Event.Manifest catalogue is an opaque JSON String using serde_json::to_string/from_str of the original typed config::Catalogue. Arbitrary manifest/configuration names may resemble descriptor markers; string opacity keeps channel scanning from interpreting them as capabilities. Catalogue parsing remains before owner descriptor extraction and maps errors through the original InvalidData helper. This transport slot does not replace manifest/domain validation.
- =Root handoff decoding= :: Command.Start and Event.Exported retain raw Value / Result<Value,String> extraction until root unknown-field rejection, then use the generated WireHandoff decoder at the original overlap/count/ownership point. Current producers use generated complete typed parameter records and standard Result<WireHandoff,String>.
- =SpawnKind= :: Generated external-tag enum retains the original SpawnKind name, Copy/Clone/Debug/Eq, default Serde unit/struct representation, open Terminal payload and absent/null exit_report behavior. The runtime reexports this metadata type; policy matches are unchanged. Canonical named producer schemas are separate from original Serde unit-map/sequence reader compatibility.
- =Process records= :: Process metadata retains full original u64/i32 structural ranges, required nullable argv/cwd/code/signal slots, environment string tuples and generated WireStdio. Descriptor duplication/consumption remains at the original stdio field point; process/signal policy and execution stay handwritten and are not narrowed by these schemas.
- =Terminal metadata= :: Generated TerminalId/TerminalStatus and terminal_metadata::{Source,Condition,Call} preserve original Rust names, traits, defaults, open struct payloads, optional emission and legacy Serde forms. Nested module avoids the existing JSON-RPC Call name. Wire numeric ranges stay native; terminal semantic limits/regex checks remain at their existing call sites.
- =Terminal result= :: The root record result remains a String containing standard Result<Value,String>. Its x-json-content-schema documents inner canonical values; actual inner parsing and errors stay at the original opaque field extraction point. Arbitrary JSON and descriptor-looking inner data stay opaque.
- =Approval metadata= :: Generated approval_metadata::{Request,Reply} preserve original closed structs, names, traits, Request package_kind default, Reply null id and omitted None message; public protocol paths reexport them. Native optional paths and strings remain wire metadata, with no approval policy validation.
- =Preview result= :: Generated WirePreview and standard Result<WirePreview,String> describe private output_path/in_store/download/build_required. Native catalog::Preview retains its public skipped output_path and domain semantics. Private producer retains its original json! path serialization panic on non-UTF8 output_path before typed conversion; missing optional named fields and legacy record/Result forms remain accepted at the original Value boundary.
- =Dev shell metadata= :: Generated devshell_metadata::{Changes,DevShellSummary} retain original names/traits/docs, open records, native u32 generation and arbitrary lock JSON. Changes Default is a Rust constructor only; lock stays required on the wire and absent update decodes None without changing explicit null output. Policy, Nix evaluation and lock handling stay native.
- =Dev shell opaque fields= :: Event.Refresh result remains a String containing standard Result<(Value,String),String>; Event.Call result contains standard Result<Value,String>; Event.DevShell summary contains original DevShellSummary JSON. x-json-content-schema documents inner canonical shapes separately. Scalar IDs parse before inner strings, inner errors before root finish; descriptor-looking inner data remains opaque.
- =Host job metadata= :: Generated HostOutcome retains original Debug/Serialize/Deserialize/Default, no added Clone, open fields, null optional exit_code/signal/spawn_error, three required bools and original status.json prose. Native policy and status-file behavior remain unchanged. Root Finished aggregate is explicit without Clone.
- =Host job results= :: Prepared and Finished carry plain standard Result<PathBuf,String> and Result<HostOutcome,String>, not opaque strings. Native paths preserve non-UTF8 fallible serialization; run arguments/environment/optional timeout retain unrestricted structural ranges and original field extraction. No host command admission, environment, outcome exclusivity or timeout policy is added.

* Envelopes (handwritten transport, schema checked)

#+begin_src json
{
  "Id": {
    "oneOf": [

        "type": "string",

        "maxLength": 64,

      },

        "type": "integer",
        "minimum": 0,

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "key": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/OperationKey"
    },
    "configuration": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchManifest"
    },

      "default": ""
    },
    "agent_name": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchAgentName"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "cwd": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/HostPath"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "scope": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchScope"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "dev_shell": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "rows": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchDimension"
    },
    "cols": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/LaunchDimension"
    },
    "detached": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "default": false
    },
    "raid": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "recreate": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "string"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "options": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/HostLaunchOptions",
      "default": {}
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "key",
    "configuration",
    "rows",
    "cols"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "description": "name is required/nonempty for an ordinary launch and empty/omitted for recreate. Recreate forbids explicit agent_name,cwd,scope,dev_shell,raid; it resolves the record and retries before ordinary validation. cwd must be absolute; dev_shell is an absolute PATH[#ATTR] reference; raid has the existing raid-name grammar. Domain validators retain diagnostics and refusal order."
}
#+end_src

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "string",
  "anyOf": [
    {
      "minLength": 1,
      "maxLength": 1,
      "not": {
        "pattern": "[\\u0000-\\u001f\\u007f-\\u009f]"
      }
    },
    {
      "pattern": "^(?:[eE][nN][tT][eE][rR]|[tT][aA][bB]|[eE][sS][cC]|[bB][aA][cC][kK][sS][pP][aA][cC][eE]|[sS][pP][aA][cC][eE]|[uU][pP]|[dD][oO][wW][nN]|[lL][eE][fF][tT]|[rR][iI][gG][hH][tT]|[hH][oO][mM][eE]|[eE][nN][dD]|[pP][aA][gG][eE][uU][pP]|[pP][aA][gG][eE][dD][oO][wW][nN]|[iI][nN][sS][eE][rR][tT]|[dD][eE][lL][eE][tT][eE]|[sS][hH][iI][fF][tT]\\+[tT][aA][bB]|[fF]1|[fF]2|[fF]3|[fF]4|[fF]5|[fF]6|[fF]7|[fF]8|[fF]9|[fF]10|[fF]11|[fF]12)$",
      "not": {
        "pattern": "[^A-Za-z0-9+]"
      }
    },
    {
      "minLength": 6,
      "maxLength": 6,
      "pattern": "^[cC][tT][rR][lL]\\+[a-zA-Z]$"
    },
    {
      "minLength": 5,
      "maxLength": 5,
      "pattern": "^[aA][lL][tT]\\+[!-~]$"
    }
  ]
}
#+end_src

** =PermissionRecord=

#+begin_src json
{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "kind": {
      "$ref": "#/definitions/PermissionRecordKind"
    },
    "id": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "session": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "agent_name": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "approval": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "package": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "reason": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "state": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "approved": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "type": "boolean"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "preview": {
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "$ref": "#/definitions/PermissionPreviewValue"
        },
        {
          "type": "null"
        }
      ]
    },
    "message": {
      "type": [
        "string",
        "null"
      ]
    },
    "name": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "started_at": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 0,
      "maximum": 18446744073709551615,
      "x-json-integer": true
    },
    "log": {
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "kind",
    "id",
    "session",
    "agent_name",
    "approval",
    "package",
    "reason",
    "state",
    "approved",
    "preview",
    "message"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true,
  "allOf": [
    {
      "if": {
        "properties": {
          "kind": {
            "const": "package"
          }
        }
      },
      "then": {
        "properties": {
          "preview": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "$ref": "#/definitions/PackageApprovalPreview"
              },
              {
                "$ref": "#/definitions/ApprovalPreviewError"
              },
              {
                "type": "null"
              }
            ]
          }
        }
      }
    },
    {
      "if": {
        "properties": {
          "kind": {
            "const": "docker"
          }
        }
      },
      "then": {
        "properties": {
          "preview": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "$ref": "#/definitions/DockerApprovalPreview"
              },
              {
                "type": "null"
              }
            ]
          }
        }
      }
    },
    {
      "if": {
        "properties": {
          "kind": {
            "const": "host-command"
          }
        }
      },
      "then": {
        "properties": {
          "preview": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "$ref": "#/definitions/HostCommandApprovalPreview"
              },
              {
                "type": "null"
              }
            ]
          }
        }
      }
    },
    {
      "if": {
        "properties": {
          "kind": {
            "const": "raid-owner"
          }
        }
      },
      "then": {
        "properties": {
          "preview": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "$ref": "#/definitions/RaidApprovalPreview"
              },
              {
                "type": "null"
              }
            ]
          }
        }
      }
    }
  ],
  "description": "Public retained permission. State is a String because private grant replies are forwarded. Required nullable approved/preview/message keys are always emitted; name/started_at/log are omitted when absent. Host log paths are on the host. Private devshell preview JSON is forwarded unchanged until C17; see PermissionPreviewValue and DevShellPreview for the current known structure."
}
#+end_src

  ],

  "description": "Daemon-authored operation or terminal-exit notice. Existing kinds include package, docker, devshell, host-command and terminal; statuses include ready, denied, failed, withdrawn, cancelled, finished and exited. Strings remain open for future producers. message is a nullable sandbox-facing explanation, never a host diagnostic."
}

    "body",
    "created_sequence",
    "created_at",
    "state",
    "claim_generation",
    "failure"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true,
  "description": "Current producer emits every first thirteen field, including nullable in_reply_to/failure, and omits absent operation/raid notices. created_at is UTC Unix milliseconds; created_sequence is authoritative. Authenticated routing supplies sender/recipient. Legacy reader retains sender_path/recipient_path aliases and original positional order; no semantic validation moves into the DTO."
}

    "worktree",

    "updated",
    "stopped",
    "running"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true,
  "description": "Public history projection; running is boolean online and explicitly null offline. Persisted Record remains a separate closed domain type without running. Load accepts versions1/2/3 and normalizes to3 in memory without rewriting; raw persistence DTO deserialization still permits future u32 versions. Canonical online result additionally requires version3 and boolean running."
}

  "description": "Compatible Rust snapshot reader: instance/sessions/permissions required; raids defaults empty and messaging defaults false/false only when absent. Emacs has a separate looser partial snapshot/integer policy."
}
