* Protocol behavior

- =framing= :: Content-Length; 256-byte headers; duplicate keys and depth >32 rejected. See docs/API.org transport rules.
- =request_id= :: 1–64 ASCII letters/digits/underscore/hyphen, or unsigned integer <=9007199254740991. No null/boolean/fractional IDs. Notifications omit ID.
- =notification_policy= :: Ordinary notifications are ignored; active exec and attach have their own exchanges.
- =connections= :: Host: 4096 calls including initialize. Sandbox: initialize then one call, up to 8 connections.
- =initialization= :: Parameters are checked before repeated-initialize conflict, then unsupported-version refusal. api accepts u32; supported version is 1. configuration is null on host and the configuration key on sandbox (null if unavailable).
- =server_control= :: Host only. Both remain available during shutdown. stop flushes acceptance then closes its connection and completes cleanup asynchronously.
- =parameter_forms= :: C01 methods accept named objects or positional arrays: initialize accepts {api:N} or [N], server methods accept {} or []. Omitted params default to {}. Preserve Serde refusal order.
- =result_extensions= :: Result objects, including nested limits and history variants, may gain fields. Clients ignore unknown result fields; known fields and history owner discrimination remain validated. Request parameters stay closed.
- =api1_compatibility= :: Producer result schemas describe the current daemon precisely. x-client-result-schema describes compatible API-1 replies accepted by clients: configuration/limits and newer server.status history/manifest/worktree metadata may be absent. Clients retain absence as optional data and use advertised features rather than inventing values.
- =self_reporting= :: sessions.status exposes only the caller and its policy/activity. Worktree paths are deliberately visible. Activity is independent of lifecycle; variables update atomically and empty values remove keys. Missing or null set-worktree name clears the record; names are validated before filesystem access.
- =session-records= :: Current session read producers emit complete endpoint-specific records. Host sessions include descriptor-free process identities and host paths; sandbox records exclude them, except the intentionally shared Worktree.path. API-1 reader schemas retain the defaults and omissions previously accepted by Rust snapshot clients. The controller retains domain records; explicit projections and a generated-read adapter preserve snapshot consumers until C15. Nested terminal and raid summaries are typed public data; their methods migrate in C05 and C11.
- =launch-lifecycle= :: Launch keys bind endpoint actor and decoded parameters; identical retries return the original acceptance, including during recreate after the record becomes reserved. Recreate record resolution precedes normal launch validation. Acceptance state starting is not readiness. Public DTOs contain no recorded-history capability or host-selectable member worktree. Sandbox start uses current owner/run-target policy and fixed member launch arguments. Stop acceptance is not completed cleanup; host resize uses an exact session id, and sandbox detach closes its one-shot connection after acceptance. Sandbox sessions.attach remains refusal-only (-32601), before its existing trailing-input handling.
- =positional-parameters= :: Method descriptors retain original Serde field order. Generated structs are named DTOs; decode_params<M> maps positional arrays to those names before generated decoding because Typify sorts struct fields. Optional omissions and trailing defaults remain the original ones. Schema defaults do not authorize or perform lifecycle transitions. Nullable fields without an original Serde default still require an array position; x-positional-required records this independently of named-object optionality. Member launch requires its third agent_name position (null is valid); set-worktree requires [name] or [null], while {} still clears it.
- =exec-descriptors= :: Host sessions.exec carries SCM_RIGHTS with the first byte of its frame: one PTY slave when tty=true, otherwise stdin/stdout/stderr in that order. No descriptor numbers appear in JSON. The existing host connection budget is three accumulated descriptors, including fragmented reads. The call takes and closes all received descriptors on refusal and transfers stdio only after validation.
- =exec-lifecycle= :: Host-only exec waits for process completion and then closes its connection. Parameter and command validation precede descriptor-count, target/pin, policy, 64-exec capacity and init-availability checks, in that order; the existing canonical init-spawn 64 KiB check remains after ID allocation and before stdio transfer. Disconnect cancels only execs on that connection. kill_execs resolves dots as slashes and sends best-effort KillAll; accepted does not mean all commands have ended.
- =exec-signal= :: exec.signal is a host-client-to-daemon notification, with no id, only during an active exec. Named {signal:I32} or positional [I32] accepts SIGHUP=1, SIGINT=2, SIGTERM=15. Missing, invalid or unsupported signals, an unexpected method, or any id close the active-exec connection; cleanup cancels its exec only. Outside active exec, all no-id client notifications are ignored after envelope parsing; a host request with id and method exec.signal is unknown (-32601). It is never a callable method or sandbox capability.
- =terminal-records= :: Terminal records expose id, nullable name, display label, host/agent origin, Unix-millisecond started_at, rows/cols and attached. IDs increase per session and are never reused; list orders started terminals oldest first, including host and agent origins. Creation answers only after Spawned; pending creates do not appear in lists. Listed records remain until the program ends. Snapshots retain their domain record bridge until C15.
- =terminal-creation= :: Host create accepts ordered positional parameters session,argv,cwd,env,name,rows,cols,notify or a closed named object; only session is required. Sandbox create/list/close accept a closed named object or null-as-empty, never positional arrays, and may never supply session. The existing sandbox relay derives the session from the connection and preserves policy/refusal ordering. Host notify=true is rejected before command/name/size validation; sandbox notify may request an exit notice. Size defaults independently to 40 rows and 120 columns. Command/name/size, target/policy/init, capacity and uniqueness checks precede canonical init encoding and spawn transfer.
- =terminal-close= :: Close requests cancellation of the terminal process group and returns accepted=true. The entry remains until exit. Sandbox callers can close only agent-created terminals; host-created ones return -32009. An explicit close suppresses exit notices; disconnect while creating does not cancel the already requested terminal. Existing lifecycle/notice ownership stays handwritten.
- =terminal-cli-fallbacks= :: Current terminal producers are precise. Legacy create CLI results read only a string id, otherwise print ?. List readers iterate only an array terminals, treat other envelopes as empty, and normalize each item independently: string identity/display fields or absent, attached only a JSON boolean, rows/cols rendered as their original JSON values (missing means null). Close readers ignore their result. Named client normalization profiles retain these behaviors around generated DTOs, including malformed fields and non-object results.
- =terminal-io-parameters= :: Host read/send/wait accept named objects or positional arrays in their field order: session,terminal then source,lines for read; text,keys for send; match,regex,exit,timeout,lines for wait. Only session and terminal are required structurally. Sandbox calls omit session and retain object-only/null-as-empty rules. Send needs a string text (even empty) or nonempty keys; empty/null text and keys are distinguished. Wait selects one nonempty match string, Rust regex string, or exit=true; match/regex allow exit absent/null/false. Source, line/timeout/text/key bounds and regex compilation remain existing semantic validators in their original order. Request constraints separately describe cross-field requirements without changing generated record/default decoding. Multi-character key names are ASCII-lowercased before modifier processing: alt+X sends ESC+x; a single-character X keeps its case. Alt modifiers accept only ASCII graphic characters (! through ~), excluding space.
- =terminal-io-results= :: Read returns text,alternate_screen,rows,cols and optional truncated=true. Wait returns matched+line, timeout, or exited+nullable code/signal+text; truncated=true is present only when output is cut. Matching final output precedes exit reporting. Existing 256 KiB text and 4 KiB line limits remain. Legacy CLI readers independently normalize string text/status/line and signed 64-bit code/signal; missing/malformed/non-object values retain their old fallback, with unknown wait status reported as timeout. Send/detach/resize callers ignore successful result bodies. Current producers use generated precise DTOs; private runtime relays stay JSON until their owning migration.
- =terminal-io-ownership= :: Read/send/wait first validate command-specific input, then resolve a live session/terminal; pending waits remain connection-owned, count toward the per-session limit of 16 and are withdrawn on disconnect. A connection serves no other call while waiting. Host detach ends only the frontend and keeps the program alive; the frontend stream remains reserved until it closes. Resize validates 1..500 rows/cols before target resolution and updates the existing screen/PTY through runtime commands. Sandbox attach/detach/resize remain unavailable and never enter its callable allowlist.
- =attach-exchanges= :: attach.sock begins with a frontend-to-daemon attach.hello notification (no id); named and ordered version,rows,cols,terminal positional parameters retain the existing Serde forms. Version 1 and nonzero u16 dimensions are checked after structural decoding; bad version or zero dimensions retain the unsupported-version diagnostic. A supplied terminal requires 1..500 dimensions during target attachment, after the existing state/policy checks. Hello has a 4 KiB body and 3-second frame deadline, eight pending hellos per goblin and at most four accepts per tick. The daemon reads exactly the hello frame and consumes no following raw bytes.
- =attach-waiting= :: While startup or required onAttach variables are pending, the daemon sends attach.waiting with a required missing array, initially/periodically at one-second intervals. Empty missing means startup/draining; nonempty names identify variables that must be fresh and nonempty this run. A live competing attachment refuses without resizing. An exited onAttach client retains its physical stream until the frontend closes; new hellos receive empty waiting notices during that drain.
- =attach-completion= :: Modern attach.sock and legacy terminal.sock complete with a correlated JSON-RPC response of id 0: result attached=true, or error -32009 with a diagnostic. Only success switches the stream to raw bytes. terminal.sock is server-first with no hello; it retains stopped output, waits for configuration/readiness and refuses onAttach goblins with an upgrade message. Modern named-terminal attachments answer at once without waiting notices and redraw before live output. The generic runtime Terminal::attach retains its private caller-supplied id (including nonzero test ids); public entrypoints pass 0. Byte queues, refusal-before-resize, descriptor ownership, cancellation and framed/raw boundaries remain handwritten.
- =attach-client-compatibility= :: The existing Rust client classifies a waiting frame by method=attach.waiting alone, without tightening its envelope or missing-item types; a nonempty params.missing array triggers Starting once, and other values mean no missing names. This named compatibility normalization produces AttachWaitingReply. Final frames retain strict existing JSON-RPC envelope/correlation checks for id 0, accept any otherwise-valid error code and require attached=true; result additions are ignored. Each waiting frame renews only the next frame first-byte deadline. STOP interrupts the wait; resize signals do not end it. Emacs invokes this host CLI and has no separate attach JSON parser.

* Common errors

- =-32700= :: Invalid JSON
- =-32600= :: Invalid frame or envelope
- =-32601= :: Method unavailable
- =-32602= :: Invalid parameters
- =-32001= :: Initialize first
- =-32002= :: Unsupported API version
- =-32003= :: Host command capacity
- =-32004= :: Record absent
- =-32009= :: Stale or conflicting operation
- =-32010= :: Resource limit or init unavailable

* Generation exceptions

- =EmptyParams= :: Typify represents empty closed objects as maps; use a handwritten deny_unknown_fields empty struct to preserve the existing Serde decoder, including [] as well as {}. Null and unknown fields remain refused.
- =required-nullable= :: Typify/Serde Option accepts absence as null. Response::decode explicitly checks required nullable result fields before deserialization. Producers emit null rather than omitting; schema response validation checks the same presence rule.
- =validation= :: Type generation is not JSON Schema validation: constraints such as byte lengths remain named handwritten validators; tests check schema agreement.
- =protocol= :: Framing, duplicate keys, request IDs, deadlines and connection state remain in libs/jsonrpc and controller; generated DTOs never authorize calls.
- =boolean-enum= :: Typify 0.7 emits bool for boolean singleton enums. Response::decode requires accepted=true. HistoryStatus uses an owner-discriminated handwritten decoder around generated record DTOs so added fields cannot select the wrong untagged variant.
- =HostPath= :: The schema string maps to std::path::PathBuf to retain existing path serialization and invalid-UTF-8 behavior. This is descriptor-free data, not runtime authority.
- =integer-representation= :: JSON Schema treats 1.0 as an integer, while existing Serde integer fields reject JSON floating-point values. x-json-integer and the Python validator preserve that representation constraint; generated Rust integer decoders enforce it naturally.
- =HistoryStatus= :: Typify does not enforce boolean singleton enums when selecting an untagged union. Select the generated OwnedHistory/UnavailableHistory record by the owner boolean, preserving unknown-field tolerance and required directory presence.
- =API-1-client-compatibility= :: Method::Produced retains the current producer DTO; Method::Result uses the generated x-client-result-schema reply DTO when older API-1 daemons omitted additive information. Optional metadata without a prior fallback stays absent. Session compatibility records explicitly preserve the defaults already used by snapshot views; direct JSON commands retain original wire presence via exchange_with_wire.
- =Variables= :: Generated parameter structs use BTreeMap<String,String>; existing SetVariables::validate enforces names/count/UTF-8 bytes/control characters in the gate and CLI, preserving diagnostics and validation order.
- =WorktreeName= :: Generated request name is String; the existing runtime worktrees::valid_name validator retains syntax checks and refusal order. Missing/null optional name still clears the record.
- =positional-parameters= :: Typify orders generated struct fields alphabetically and gives optional object fields Serde defaults. decode_params<M> maps positional arrays using generated PARAM_ORDER and MIN_POSITIONAL before generated named decoding. OpenRPC x-positional-required records nullable fields that the original sequence decoder required despite named-object optionality. Legacy reference decoders prove original field mapping, acceptance and default values. Positional array schemas stay in x-positional-schemas rather than generating tuple DTOs.
- =result-shapes= :: Serde structs accept positional arrays even for result objects. Response::decode applies validate_result_shape recursively using the checked-in schema to require object/array kinds, required field presence and union discriminants before generated deserialization. This is a structural guard, not a general JSON Schema validator: other constraints retain their generated or named validators. Optional API-1 metadata remains optional and nullable.
- =session-record-bridges= :: Controller lifecycle/domain records retain their ownership and existing types. HostSession/SandboxSession and SelfStatus explicitly project descriptor-free identity/worktree/creator/raid/terminal data and parse trusted lifecycle/origin strings into generated wire enums. The snapshot reader decodes generated HostSessionReply before converting to the legacy TUI/domain view until C15; its existing defaults and docker_scope alias/conflict rules remain unchanged. Terminal and raid method migrations retire their corresponding summary bridges in C05/C11.
- =cli-json-output= :: Generated compatibility DTOs model older omission/default rules and ignore result extensions; reserializing them would lose original null/presence/extension information. exchange_with_wire validates through the associated generated result DTO then returns the original result alongside it for the JSON list commands. Readers use the typed value; JSON output uses only the already-validated original result.
- =launch-validation= :: OperationKey, LaunchConfigurationName, LaunchAgentName, LaunchScope and LaunchManifest map to String; LaunchDimension maps to u16; option maps stay BTreeMap<String,String>. Existing controller/gate validators retain static bounds, policy checks, recreate/retry ordering and diagnostics instead of moving checks into generated deserialization. Raw decoding cases are distinct from schema-valid cases where a named semantic validator is required.
- =launch-retry-cache= :: The controller retains its serialized acceptance cache and domain Start (including private recorded history and member worktree). Generated HostLaunchParams projects into Start; HostLaunchResult produces the acceptance. Sandbox projection decodes that known cached result into the generated DTO and emits only MemberLaunchResult fields; no host paths cross the sandbox launch reply.
- =host-launch-omission= :: Typify cannot emit skip_serializing_if for a replaced BTreeMap field. Method OMIT_EMPTY_OBJECTS comes from x-client-omit-empty-objects and params_value<M> omits the empty host options object before a typed exchange. Nonempty options remain present and reach the existing host refusal; inbound decoding/defaults are unchanged. Strict pre-launch-options fake-daemon CLI tests cover run and resume.
- =client-param-presence= :: ParamPresence metadata comes from x-client-param-presence. params_value<M> retains host run null agent_name/scope/dev_shell, removes the empty name for recreate, and retains sandbox run null agent_name. It never removes a nonempty supplied name or other explicit values, so invalid combinations still reach existing validation. This is caller wire-shape compatibility around generated DTOs; inbound decoding is unchanged.
- =exec-command-validation= :: ExecText, ExecArguments and ExecDirectory map to String/Vec<String>/String; ExecEnvironment maps to Vec<(String,String)>. Existing validate_command retains UTF-8 byte, NUL, argv/env bounds and name/path diagnostics before descriptor/authority checks. The canonical init encoding validator remains after target, policy, capacity and init checks. Descriptor transport/ownership and cancellation remain handwritten.
- =exec-signal= :: ExecSignalNumber maps to i32 so the existing forwardable validator retains legal signal/state handling. Generated notification identity and ordered parameters are separate from callable Method identities. Invalid active-exec input closes instead of returning an RPC error.
- =exec-client-reply= :: ExecReply is the generated normalized client DTO, not a restriction on old wire results. The named exec-field-fallback Response adapter preserves the original CLI: code/signal are independently read as signed 64-bit JSON integers, message as a string; malformed/missing fields become None. Non-object results have no recognized fields, retaining the exit-255 fallback. x-client-wire-result-schema explicitly accepts these historical inputs; current producer schemas remain precise. Canonical JSON-RPC envelope/correlation errors are still rejected.
- =terminal-parameter-validation= :: TerminalName maps to String and TerminalDimension to u16. Existing controller validators retain byte/syntax/size checks, host-notify refusal, command checks and diagnostic/order semantics. Sandbox terminal calls preserve null-as-empty and object-only semantics independently of host positional forms; no new parameter validation moves ahead of the existing sandbox policy gate.
- =terminal-domain-bridge= :: The domain TerminalRecord retains mutable lifecycle state and snapshot equality/serialization until C15. A single From<&TerminalRecord> projection emits generated SessionTerminal for create/list and session projections. Runtime capabilities and pending creation/notice state do not enter the public DTO.
- =terminal-cli-normalization= :: Current terminal producers are precise. Legacy create CLI results read only a string id, otherwise print ?. List readers iterate only an array terminals, treat other envelopes as empty, and normalize each item independently: string identity/display fields or absent, attached only a JSON boolean, rows/cols rendered as their original JSON values (missing means null). Close readers ignore their result. Named client normalization profiles retain these behaviors around generated DTOs, including malformed fields and non-object results.
- =terminal-io-validation= :: TerminalReadSource, TerminalKey, TerminalRegex and TerminalSendText map to String; TerminalLines to usize; TerminalTimeout to u64; TerminalKeys to Vec<String>. Existing source matching, key_bytes, terminal::validate and Rust regex compilation preserve syntax, byte/count/line/timeout bounds, cross-field choices and diagnostics/refusal order. x-request-constraints publishes cross-field JSON Schema separately from generated structural DTOs. Rust regex syntax/compiled size remains a named semantic constraint, not a JSON Schema format.
- =terminal-io-producers= :: Supervisor screen/read and matched/timeout/exited/send producers construct generated public DTOs, then retain their existing Value runtime envelope until C17. Controller parameter decoding and command ownership/cancellation stay handwritten; no private descriptor or lifecycle transport is changed.
- =attach-validation= :: AttachVersion maps to u64 and AttachDimension to u16. Existing hello version/nonzero checks retain unsupported-version versus invalid-parameter diagnostics. Named-terminal 1..500 sizing remains after existing state/policy checks. Generated notification decoders retain positional hello order; waiting producers are named only.
- =attach-waiting-compatibility= :: The existing Rust client classifies a waiting frame by method=attach.waiting alone, without tightening its envelope or missing-item types; a nonempty params.missing array triggers Starting once, and other values mean no missing names. This named compatibility normalization produces AttachWaitingReply. Final frames retain strict existing JSON-RPC envelope/correlation checks for id 0, accept any otherwise-valid error code and require attached=true; result additions are ignored. Each waiting frame renews only the next frame first-byte deadline. STOP interrupts the wait; resize signals do not end it. Emacs invokes this host CLI and has no separate attach JSON parser.
- =attach-runtime-id= :: The generic runtime Terminal::attach keeps its caller-supplied id and byte-queue API. Public modern/legacy entrypoints pass the fixed 0 id; generated completion payload DTOs are used without rewriting private nonzero-id support. Public completion consumers and refusal constructors bind to generated exchange metadata.
- =flake-app-validation= :: FlakeAppName maps to String. Existing FlakeRunParams::validate retains ASCII syntax, byte bounds, policy order and diagnostics after generated structural decoding. Domain evaluation, mount authority and cancellation remain handwritten.
- =devshell-client-normalization= :: Current lock/run producers are precise. API-1 clients independently read string path/program; absent or malformed values preserve missing lock path/missing program diagnostics. Lock generation uses as_u64 with fallback 0, lock contents remain arbitrary JSON with missing treated as null. Named normalization profiles retain these behaviors even for non-object wire results. flake.run keeps its feature gate and no response deadline; the existing store_program validator still checks normalized /nix/store paths and file existence.
- =devshell-generation= :: DevShellGeneration maps to u32, retaining the full existing runtime range. Launch starts at 1; refresh uses unchecked u32 addition, which can wrap to zero in release builds. The contract does not add overflow checks or change runtime behavior.
- =devshell-preview-domain= :: Generated DevShellPreview and nested records replace wire-only supervisor records; the internal generic Change<T> calculation remains domain data and projects explicitly into generated input/package change records. Attention, incoming/change/kind discriminants remain trusted producer strings or generated variants. Evaluation state, candidate ownership, opaque flake locks and private Value runtime envelopes stay with their existing owners until C17.
- =devshell-preview-validation= :: DevShellInputs maps to Vec<String>; input_name and DevShellDiffParams::validate retain byte/count/syntax bounds, update/lock exclusivity and error/policy order. PreviewCount maps to usize; generation retains the full u32 range. Source diffs remain bounded/truncated by the existing producer, and incoming lists are cut to 1000 without a truncation marker.
- =devshell-preview-client= :: DevShellPreviewReply is a generated normalized display projection, separate from precise current producers. Legacy diff clients accept any object and reject non-objects. Missing/malformed lists become empty; list items independently retain recognized string/boolean/unsigned fields with old fallbacks. PreviewNumber carries both the original JSON display and an optional as_u64 value because the CLI and TUI intentionally format malformed numeric fields differently. Missing numeric fields display null in the TUI and ? in CLI count fields; TUI build defaults to zero. Locked sides are present only for objects; version sides only for arrays, preserving missing versus empty and each invalid member fallback. Unknown attention kinds remain readable. The TUI projects generic permission preview values at the display boundary until C09; Emacs retains its plist reader with shared contract fixtures and existing escaping tests.
- =editor-validation= :: EditorPath maps to String, EditorPosition to u32 and EditorFiles to Vec<EditorFile>. editor::Open::validate retains 1..32 file count, UTF-8 path bounds, control/component checks, positive positions and column-needs-line diagnostics before editor policy. CLI lexical normalization does not resolve symlinks. Generated request DTOs never construct host filenames or select another session.
- =nested-positional-parameters= :: Typify alphabetically orders fields, including nested record fields. x-wire-params-schema models the accepted named request with object-or-positional file records; x-params-schema models its normalized generated DTO. x-nested-positional-params publishes original order/minimum for array items. decode_params maps only these declared nested arrays before generated named decoding. The checker verifies metadata against the nested record, shared positional schema and wire schema. Legacy decoder comparison covers mixed named/positional records, absence/null/defaults, counts and malformed forms.
- =editor-client-result= :: Current producers emit i32 code and omit message on success; nonzero exit emits the existing filtered diagnostic bounded to 512 characters. EditorOpenReply normalizes code independently as signed i64 and message as a string, otherwise absent, retaining CLI code fallback 1 and the original i64-to-i32 cast. Non-object results retain these fallbacks. Canonical JSON-RPC envelope/correlation checks, feature gate and patient wait remain unchanged.
- =editor-producer-dependency= :: The strict canonical producer schema uses if/then/else to require message for nonzero code and forbid it for zero code. Typify 0.7 rejects conditional schemas; build.rs removes only this named dependency from its temporary generation input, retaining the structural DTO and every other schema field. Edit::outcome preserves the existing branch enforcing the dependency. Strict schema corpora and actual outcome tests check both branches; independent legacy client normalization is unchanged.
- =permission-decision-domain= :: The generated decision request replaces the local wire struct, including internal inherited-approval construction; the host endpoint still supplies actor and the existing controller owns all record/state/preview/requester/queue checks. Public request strings retain legacy unrestricted structural decoding before lookup; no identifier check moves ahead of authority or stale-state checks.
- =permission-decision-client= :: PermissionDecisionResult precisely produces accepted=true and echoed request/approved. Existing frontends ignore result fields for success; a named compatibility projection independently reads boolean accepted/approved and string request into generated PermissionDecisionReply, otherwise absent, accepting historical non-objects as before. The host client returns typed reply plus the original validated wire value so the plain approval frontend preserves its exact JSON presentation, including additions and absence. TUI and Emacs retain their ignored-result behavior.
- =permission-request-validation= :: PermissionKind, PermissionPackage, HostCommandText/Directory/Name/EnvName and grant state strings retain String decoding; command args/env map to Vec<String>/Vec<(String,String)> and timeout/code to u64/i32. Existing PermissionParams::validate, HostCommand::validate, host_name, package_name and input_name retain syntax/count/byte/cross-field checks and diagnostics before policy. JSON Schema and the x-host-command-bytes validator describe the same restrictions without moving authority or path resolution into generated DTOs.
- =permission-result-discriminant= :: Typify untagged result selection cannot safely choose a failed host outcome versus a plain failed grant because result objects permit additions. A named PermissionRequestResult wrapper selects generated outcome records by reserved host fields, then pending status, else grant; canonical oneOf/forbidden-field constraints retain precise required/null shapes. Producers construct the generated branch records. Forwarded private grant status strings stay String until the private protocol migration; the canonical plain status schema therefore remains a string, including future values. Required message presence and reserved host fields distinguish plain pending/finished statuses from acceptance and host-result branches. The strict wrapper also enforces PermissionHostLog prefix, nonempty slash-free request component and exact output.log suffix because its String replacement does not enforce the canonical pattern. Both legacy client projections remain unchanged.
- =permission-client-profiles= :: One method has two existing readers. Its default generated PermissionRequestReply projection preserves the host CLI independent string request/status/message and signed-i64 code fallbacks, including non-objects. The generated grant-cli profile binds the same method to PermissionGrantReply, preserving closed fields, named message omission, positional request/status/message order, identifier and ready/denied/error checks. This profile emits message:null as before; RPC-error JSON presentation retains the original validated error object. No valid producer schema is weakened for legacy readers.
- =permission-request-presence= :: The named outbound permission-request-fields profile preserves package presence for package requests and omission of default empty package for other kinds, outer false lock omission, and host env:[] plus omission of false detach. Explicit nonempty values are retained. Typed request structures remain separate from caller wire-presence normalization.
- =permission-record-domain-bridge= :: PermissionRecordKind retains String construction; private grant state and legacy kind/state remain String. PermissionPreviewValue maps to serde_json::Value, nullable in records: public leaf producers construct generated package/error/Docker/host/raid DTOs; private Event::Refresh forwards arbitrary JSON until C17. The canonical record has kind-specific preview constraints. Typify cannot generate if/then, so only PermissionRecord.allOf is removed from the temporary generator input; generated leaf DTOs and existing producer branches enforce those known forms without adding a new runtime refusal. Local controller lifecycle/snapshot records stay domain-owned; public read projections and a generated compatible snapshot reader bridge them until C15.
- =permission-record-legacy-reader= :: The generated PermissionRecordReply retains required string identity/subject/state fields, kind default package, open additions, arbitrary preview JSON and optional nullable approved/preview/message/name/started_at/log fields. A named reader maps original positional [kind,id,session,agent_name,approval,package,reason,state,approved,preview,message,name?,started_at?,log?] with minimum 11 before generated Serde decoding; list and snapshot readers apply it element-wise. Normalized DTO serialization emits approved/preview/message:null and omits absent name/started_at/log as before.
- =host-job-result-discriminant= :: HostJob maps to a named wrapper over generated HostJobActive/Completed records, selecting a completed shape when any exit_code/signal/code key is present, checking required fields and the sandbox log path. Both records retain status String because the permission record overrides cached state. Host outcome cache uses the existing generated PermissionHostOutcome DTO; view construction preserves the old cache/default plus record override behavior.
- =ignored-result-reader= :: IgnoredResult is a named empty struct replacing Typify map generation. Its Response decoder always returns the empty projection, preserving clients which ignore successful list/cancel bodies while returning the original validated JSON for printing. It is not a producer schema and does not weaken current result validation.
- =host-submitted-legacy-reader= :: HostSubmittedReply is generated but a named projection retains the original method-only classification, string params.request or empty fallback, including nonstandard envelopes and malformed/missing params. Canonical notifications still have jsonrpc 2.0, no id and closed named request params.
- =raid-domain-and-legacy-records= :: RaidNameRule maps to String so generated request and result DTOs do not move syntax or authority checks earlier. Existing pure raid functions retain name/role/list validation, membership and generation invariants. RaidLegacyOptions maps to BTreeMap<String,String>. Local mutable raid/member records remain domain-owned until C15; membership data wraps generated SessionRaid in C11b; generated record projections and a compatible snapshot reader bridge them. The compatible reader maps original record and nested member positional orders, keeps unknown fields/states/roles, and defaults missing owner/role/created_by/former_owner to null, paused false, generation zero and options {} exactly as the original Serde structs did.
- =raid-display-reader= :: RaidDisplayReply/RaidMemberDisplay are generated normalized display DTOs. A named projection independently reads string fields or -, boolean flags, members only when an array and string-created_by presence. Options retain the existing launch_choices escaping, sorted keys, non-string ? fallback and comma-spacing replacement. Raw JSON is retained for JSON printing and the compatibility formatter; sandbox callers now bind the generated member display result.
- =raid-owner-ui-param-profile= :: The TUI request profile preserves raw shown id/generation/member-id JSON and explicit null guard keys rather than coercing malformed legacy preview values into absent guards. Generated method-bound ParamsProfile types carry those fields as Value while raid remains String and optional stop_members is emitted only when true. Canonical host params remain strict String/u64, so malformed requests still receive the old receiver refusals. The profile does not grant authority.
- =raid-membership-generated-domain-wrapper= :: Local RaidMembership is a transparent wrapper around generated SessionRaid data with its former Eq/PartialEq traits. A named legacy decoder maps original [id,name,role,owner,paused?] order/minimum four and old named missing nullable/paused defaults through SessionRaidReply before constructing SessionRaid. This preserves direct and nested legacy deserialization while removing C02 field-by-field copies; mutable raid ownership still lives in the domain.
- =mailbox-serde-records= :: Generated message, notice and mutation DTOs retain schema-declared original Rust struct names with schema-name aliases for exposed malformed-request diagnostics and original field order for direct Serde sequence readers; nullable fields without old Serde defaults keep their positional requirement and serialized nulls. Message reader aliases sender_path and recipient_path remain accepted while current producers emit sender_name/recipient_name. The generator edits the Rust syntax tree, checks every field/order/alias and preserves all unrelated generated code. Semantic byte limits, identifiers, claims, routing, idempotency and authority stay in the existing mutation/state machine.
- =mailbox-result-constraints= :: Only MailboxNextResult and MailboxCompleteResult allOf constraints are removed from the temporary generator input; canonical schemas retain coherent null/non-null claim pairs, claimed message state, matching generations via x-equal-fields, and completed state. Existing producers select matching message/generation values and completed state, with independent producer wire-equivalence tests. CLI successful bodies still use IgnoredResult/raw JSON rather than imposing a new legacy refusal. Draft 7 cross-instance-value equality is declared through x-equal-fields and checked by the repository contract validator. Read-side typed projections, adapter inbox handling and unfinished-operation/health records remain C12b.
- =mailbox-status-readers= :: InboxStatusReply requires all five original core fields so incomplete replies become read errors instead of quiet/empty inboxes. Additive raid_audit_failed/operations default false/empty for older peers. Adapters validate typed RPC replies then adapt them to their existing persisted JSON ledger format; ledger storage/lifecycle stays domain-owned. InboxHead is a named (String,u64) alias because Typify replacement paths cannot be tuple expressions. MailboxStatusParams uses a named empty Empty struct, as closed empty maps otherwise change exposed original Serde diagnostics.
- =communications-envelope-stage= :: C13a generates the existing event envelope and exact Serde name/order; kind/data remain String/Value until C13b introduces producer payload pairing and compatible retained-log consumers. CommunicationsLimit maps to usize, retaining the old decoder. Request emit-defaults removes only serialization omission, preserving after=0/limit=100 read defaults; session retains its original required positional nullable slot. The CLI outbound profile emits after/session and omits limit exactly as before.
- =communications-page-reader= :: The CLI page reader normalizes fields independently: events only as array of raw JSON values, cursor/latest only as u64, session only as string. Missing/malformed values remain absent so the caller retains original event printing and page/cursor/sequence error order; successful legacy bodies gain no new early refusal. The canonical producer result stays strict. C13b owns event-data consumers.
- =communications-typed-payloads= :: Generated CommunicationKind markers bind each known kind to its DTO; CommunicationPayload keeps its fields private and exposes only a paired kind/data result. Raid and operation domain enums retain authority and map into these DTOs; public stored envelopes remain compatible String/Value readers. Canonical kind/data and delivered/undelivered/correlation constraints stay in JSON schema; only CommunicationsEvent, CommRaidNoticeData, CommOperationCompletedData, CommMessageAcceptedChange and CommMessageRepliedChange allOf clauses are omitted from the generator clone because Typify cannot express conditionals/cross-field relations. Existing producer branches and independent original-wire comparisons enforce runtime construction. Mutation markers bind their method-specific wrapper and generated change DTO; retry IDs are projected from internally generated cached results, retaining null when no ID exists. Session and recipient producers use generated bindings too. Compatible readers are separate from strict producer DTOs: the offline reader projects only independent string fields into CommunicationSessionStartedRead; generated lazy Emacs record views retain raw plist values and original evaluation/errors. Raw stored payloads remain intentionally open for unknown, malformed or partial historical data. Producer registry and source checks cover all22 known kinds. Canonical accepted/replied schemas preserve newly created message state/zero generation/null failure, accepted host/null versus raid/string correlation and null in_reply_to, and replied in_reply_to/completed equality. Requeue reason remains nonempty with a 512-byte UTF-8 limit; generated String replacements leave the existing Mutation validator authoritative.
- =history-persistence-and-readers= :: Public history records are generated projections from the existing closed persisted Record domain type; persisted loader/version normalization/writer/order/locks and recreation reservations remain handwritten. No file rewrite. Prune generated parameters retain original Prune name/field order/default emission; selection remains a local extension trait. HistoryListResult canonical version3/online-boolean-running allOf is omitted only in Typify input; producer follows the original store/live state. Offline generated projection emits running:null. Rust display/list/prune readers project legacy fields independently, preserve raw record arrays and failed rows, preserve failed-before-removed Serde diagnostics and original fallback/printing behavior. Lazy generated Emacs field views retain partial/malformed record behavior. The two host history methods explicitly use generated direct-Serde parameter decoding, with preserved DTO field order/default slots. Positional arrays are not normalized to objects, preserving original excess-length messages and first-invalid-field precedence; other endpoint methods keep their existing decoding strategy.
- =state-snapshot-and-stream-readers= :: Strict StateSnapshot/current subscription and notification records are generated separately from the compatible Snapshot reader. Generated nested field hooks preserve original row validation and error order, including duplicate scope aliases, required agent_name and valid AgentState before domain conversion. Required top-level sessions/permissions are not defaulted; absent raids/health are defaulted and still emitted. UI/domain Snapshot remains a local view converted from the generated reader. Initial reply and tick projections retain raw snapshots for validation at the original point; initial cross-instance acceptance, no-data/EOF/budget/frame sequencing, partial commits and stream-drop behavior stay handwritten. Lazy Emacs records and generated identities retain partial snapshot/signed integer behavior. state.subscribe/unsubscribe use original direct-Serde params to preserve dispatch diagnostics. C15b separately repairs the independently confirmed earlier sessions.resize positional-diagnostic regression; it is not an ignored test or a claim of whole-public diagnostic equivalence.
